# Skills · C · part 333

839 entries, cross-sample-component-area-aggregation to crosscheck-lanes. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **cross-sample-component-area-aggregation**: Use when a GC-MS hit table exported from vendor peak-detection software (e.g. Agilent Unknowns Analysis) contains multiple occurrences of the same tentative compound across samples and per-compound component areas must be collected and standardized. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-sample-component-area-aggregation))
- **cross-sample-feature-alignment**: Use when you have run mass detection and chromatogram building independently on each LC-MS/MS sample and produced per-sample feature lists with m/z, retention time, and intensity values. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/cross-sample-feature-alignment) · 2 copies)
- **cross-sample-feature-alignment**: Use when you have peak/feature tables per sample from multidimensional MS acquisitions and need one feature list matched across all study samples, QC pools, and blanks using simultaneous m/z, drift time, and retention time tolerances. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-sample-feature-alignment))
- **cross-sample-feature-grouping**: Use when you have per-sample deconvolution results from untargeted GC×GC–MS data (e.g. GcDUO Module III output) and need to merge duplicate features into cross-sample consensus features with consensus spectra before annotation or integration. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-sample-feature-grouping))
- **cross-sample-feature-matching**: Use when you have detected feature tables from two or more LC-IMS-MS/MS samples and need to establish correspondence between features across samples to enable quantitative comparison, statistical analysis, or consensus feature calling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/cross-sample-feature-matching) · 3 copies)
- **cross-sample-group-difference-estimation**: Use when running the TIC peak detection stage of AutoTuner and you must supply the Group difference value, the sole parameter estimated through a manual, non-automatable cross-sample comparison rather than statistical inference on raw data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-sample-group-difference-estimation))
- **cross-sample-hmp-contig-merge-evaluation**: Use when you have independently assembled contig FASTA files (any number of input Multi-FASTA sets) from related metagenome samples and want to merge them via OLC-based assembly into one improved contig set and evaluate contiguity gains. - **cross-sample-label-transfer**: Use when you have an annotated single-cell/nuclei RNA-seq dataset (e.g., .h5ad with 'class_label' in AnnData.obs) and need to predict cell-type labels on cells from a different donor or sample using SIMS label transfer. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-sample-label-transfer))
- **cross-sample-metabolite-matching**: Use when after feature extraction (MS1 peak picking, MS2 recognition, or targeted list extraction) from multiple individual samples and before annotation. - **cross-sample-strain-tracking**: Use when shotgun metagenomes from related hosts (e.g. mother-infant pairs, household cohabitants) have been profiled with MetaPhlAn and you need to determine whether the same strain of a species — including uncharacterized uSGBs like Lachnospiraceae SGB4894 — is shared between samples. - **cross-sample-transcript-sequence-matching**: Use when two or more samples' reconstructed transcript sets (e.g., IsoCon vs ICE outputs as FASTA) must be intersected by exact sequence identity, or when counting exact nucleotide matches to known reference CDSs (e.g., 61 Ensembl coding sequences of nine Y ampliconic gene families). Se usa para reporting ad hoc, exploración, POCs o análisis que requieren consultar datos externos sin ingerirlos primero en Databricks. ([aleazofeifam/Bricksdemos](https://github.com/aleazofeifam/Bricksdemos/tree/HEAD/skills/Data%20Analyst/cross-source-federation-analyst))
- **cross-source-gap-analysis**: Compare findings across Slack, GitHub, NVIDIA forums, and Outlook to identify alignment gaps, missing coverage, and follow-ups. ([NVIDIA/nemoclaw-community](https://github.com/NVIDIA/nemoclaw-community/tree/HEAD/examples/recipes/nvidia/developer-community-chief-of-staff/agents/hermes/skills/cross-source-gap-analysis) · ★146 · 4 copies)
- **cross-source-investigation**: Investigates Microsoft product changes, including upcoming or past-due roadmap items, tenant impact, known issues, and technical questions across Release Communications, Enterprise, and Microsoft Learn while preserving each source's authority. ([saurabhkoshta/Admin-Updates-Agents](https://github.com/saurabhkoshta/Admin-Updates-Agents/tree/HEAD/behaviors/cross-source-investigation) · ★1)
- **cross-source-match-and-act**: Applies when entities from one source need to be filtered or acted upon based on matching identifiers found in another source. ([KL0820/simsa-appworld-agent](https://github.com/KL0820/simsa-appworld-agent/tree/HEAD/data/mind_skill/skills/release/thesis_final/code_planner/best/6ea6792_2/cross-source-match-and-act))
- **cross-source-metabolite-frequency-filtering**: Use when you have a merged/harmonized metabolite table from multiple information sources (e.g. an MMS InChIKey-merged list) and need to reduce false positives before pathway enrichment by keeping only compounds supported by ≥2 sources. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-source-metabolite-frequency-filtering))
- **cross-source-metabolite-harmonization**: Use when multiple metabolite datasets from different sources (e.g. Metabolomics Workbench studies, HMDB, literature tables) must be merged on a common key and annotated with identifiers and ontology for intercomparison. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-source-metabolite-harmonization))
- **cross-source-ordering-test-identity-link**: Before registering or running any test that compares an ORDER, RANK, or SEQUENCE across two data sources, verify a per-INDIVIDUAL identity link exists on BOTH sides — pairing two sorted sequences by rank when the individual mapping is unknown is an algebraic identity that returns Spearman rho = 1.0 on every unit regardless of what either source encodes. Use when a plan proposes "does source A's sequence predict source B's order", when validating a vendor listing order against an observed speaking/arrival/execution order, when matching counts on both sides is being treated as sufficient to run… ([xuxiguo/research-factory-claude](https://github.com/xuxiguo/research-factory-claude/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/cross-source-ordering-test-identity-link))
- **cross-source-pair-merge-deduplication**: Use when building or extending a ligand-receptor reference database from multiple prior L-R resources and you must reconcile, deduplicate, and verify literature support per pair before publishing the merged set. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-source-pair-merge-deduplication))
- **cross-source-pathway-mapping**: Use when you need to measure how much metabolite content is shared between pathway definitions from different databases (e.g., KEGG vs Reactome vs WikiPathways) to assess redundancy before enrichment analysis or pathway clustering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-source-pathway-mapping))
- **cross-source-protein-sequence-integration**: Use when constructing a reference protein database from coding genes of collected genomes plus curated entries (e.g. UniProt fungal subset), and redundancy and annotation quality must be controlled. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-source-protein-sequence-integration))
- **cross-source-queries**: Cross-source analysis for detecting patterns across data sources. Includes intention-reality gap detection, commitment accountability tracking, and serendipity/convergence analysis. Reads vault.toml and queries normalized SQLite databases (see SCHEMAS.md). ([shippy/personal-intelligence-kit](https://github.com/shippy/personal-intelligence-kit/tree/HEAD/template/.claude/skills/cross-source-queries) · ★14)
- **cross-source-reconcile**: Discipline for reconciling parser output against a SECOND independent source (Hourly GSheet vs Revenue SSOT, COL vs Master, GrabFood vs SSOT). Prevents false "gap" alarms caused by stale/pre-ingest cached cross-checks. Use whenever a parser emits numbers that must agree with another SSOT/file before being reported or committed. ([Warren-Githut/personal-os-automation](https://github.com/Warren-Githut/personal-os-automation/tree/HEAD/personal_vault/.scripts/skills/data-science/cross-source-reconcile) · 2 copies)
- **cross-source-reconciliation**: When setting Meta's claimed purchases and revenue against banked orders from Shopify and GA4 — the mandatory reconciliation that runs before any economic conclusion. Use when the user asks "why doesn't Meta match Shopify," "Meta says 400 sales but we only got 250," "which number do I trust," "blended MER," or whenever a ROAS is about to be used to justify a decision. Produces order claim ratio, value claim share, blended MER, implied AOV and modelled share. For the modelling mechanics behind the gap, see modeled-conversions. ([undark-ai/Meta-Ads-OS](https://github.com/undark-ai/Meta-Ads-OS/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/cross-source-reconciliation))
- **cross-source-research**: Multi-source investigation workflow for researching topics across configured MCP tools (Slack, Confluence, Jira, GitHub, Drive, databases, analytics). Enforces depth over breadth by requiring content to be read, not just referenced. ([bobmatnyc/claude-mpm](https://github.com/bobmatnyc/claude-mpm/tree/HEAD/src/claude_mpm/skills/bundled/main/cross-source-research) · ★159)

- **cross-source-verify**: Движок круговой сверки: берёт результаты из НЕСКОЛЬКИХ источников (агрегаторы, госреестры, веб, выгрузки) и сводит в один ответ с дедупликацией, оценкой уверенности (свежесть × авторитет × согласие источников), tier-маркерами и явным показом расхождений. Адаптация enterprise-search/knowledge-synthesis под РФ-источники + опыт мульти-агрегаторной проверки. - **cross-source-verify**: Движок круговой сверки: берёт результаты из НЕСКОЛЬКИХ источников (агрегаторы, госреестры, веб, выгрузки) и сводит в один ответ с дедупликацией, оценкой уверенности (свежесть × авторитет × согласие источников), tier-маркерами и явным показом расхождений. - **cross-spec-consistency-check**: Pre-authoring conflict detection across the specs in a build's source-grounding set — API naming drift, double authority, schema drift, authority overlap. Triggers when a build run has 2+ source specs to check before authoring (build specify); NOT for a greenfield spec with no source set. ([NashEQify/forge](https://github.com/NashEQify/forge/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/cross-spec-consistency-check) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **cross-spec-review**: Use when spec-review passed and docs/spp/04-specs/ contains more than one spec - reviews the whole spec set for interface consistency, seam gaps, contradictions and build order ([tsergeytovarov/super-puper-powers](https://github.com/tsergeytovarov/super-puper-powers/tree/HEAD/skills/cross-spec-review) · ★10)
- **cross-spec-review**: Review a SET of specs against each other for mutual coherence — the coherence lens (audit-coherence) raised from one change to the set of specs an epic produced in parallel. Catches inter-spec conflicts before any of them is implemented: overlapping or duplicated scope, contradictory decisions, colliding API/naming surface, one spec assuming what another removes, and cross-issue dependency collisions. Read-only — returns a ranked conflict report to the caller (normally epic-lifecycle), which walks the user through the decisions and routes the alignment. Runs on specs the user has already appr… ([fixpoint-labs/flow-state-dev](https://github.com/fixpoint-labs/flow-state-dev/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/cross-spec-review) · ★2)
- **cross-spec-review**: Review a release's specs AGAINST EACH OTHER before implementation — contradictions, double ownership, unmeetable requirements, gaps, vocabulary forks, and shared chokepoints. Gate 1 reviews one change at a time and structurally cannot find these. Use BEFORE /opsx:apply on any release holding 2+ spec files, after any concurrent amendment of several specs, and whenever a release bundles more than one capability. Triggers: "cross-spec review", "do the specs agree", "release-level gate", "review the specs together", "we have more than one spec". ([cfdude/pm](https://github.com/cfdude/pm/tree/HEAD/skills/cross-spec-review) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **cross-spec-validation**: Use when a new or changed spec may contradict or supersede an existing one. Keywords: contradiction, spec conflict, supersede, breaking change, API contract, schema conflict. Do NOT use for single-spec review (feature-spec-review). ([jansalwowski/myspec](https://github.com/jansalwowski/myspec/tree/HEAD/skills/cross-spec-validation) · ★2)
- **cross-specialist-integration**: Coordinate cross-specialist integration. Verify contracts between API, frontend, database, and auth layers. Run integration tests and create fix tasks for breaking changes. Triggers: integration, cross-team, connect services, wire up, end-to-end integration ([knofler/myai](https://github.com/knofler/myai/tree/HEAD/skills/cross-specialist-integration) · ★1 · 7 copies)
- **cross-species-aging-atlas-analysis**: Use when you need to reproduce or extend cell-type-by-age analyses of cellular senescence in single-cell atlases spanning multiple ages/species, e.g. scoring cells with SenePy signatures and comparing old vs young marker-positive proportions per cell type. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-aging-atlas-analysis))
- **cross-species-aiso-consensus-analysis**: Use when short-read RNA-seq datasets plus genome FASTA and GTF annotations are available for multiple species and the goal is to identify intron-exon features (splice-site strength, intron length, branch point-to-AG distance) universally associated with AISO patterns. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-species-aiso-consensus-analysis))
- **cross-species-aiso-conservation-assessment**: Use when you have RNA-seq FASTQ pairs from replicate, cell-type, tissue, species, or spliceosomal-disruption contrasts (e.g. K562/HeLa, mouse brain vs liver, human vs mouse brain, SF3B1-drug vs DMSO, GSE207459) and need to test whether AISO patterns around exons remain stable across the pairs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-species-aiso-conservation-assessment))
- **cross-species-benchmark-comparison**: Use when you have de novo sequencing predictions on annotated MS/MS spectra from several species or heterogeneous samples and need a standardized peptide- and amino-acid-level accuracy comparison across models or training datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-species-benchmark-comparison))
- **cross-species-benchmark-peptide-precision-evaluation**: Use when comparing two de novo sequencing models (e.g., two Casanovo variants trained on different training sets) with a controlled cross-species evaluation, or when reporting peptide precision and average precision on held-out species from MSV000090982. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-species-benchmark-peptide-precision-evaluation))
- **cross-species-benchmark-reproduction**: Use when scRNA-seq FASTQs from multiple organisms must be assessed for how much transcriptional signal falls outside current genome annotations, or when reproducing reported uTAR read fractions (e.g., hg38 2%, lemur 7.1%, mole rat 4.2%, sea urchin 10.7%). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-benchmark-reproduction))
- **cross-species-cell-fate-annotation-transfer**: Use when a query scRNA-seq dataset from one species must receive continuous cell fate (differentiation potential) annotations from a curated reference dataset in another species, requiring cross-batch and cross-species distribution alignment. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-cell-fate-annotation-transfer))
- **cross-species-cell-type-annotation**: Use when training labels exist only in one species (e.g., mouse Tabula Muris) and you must annotate scRNA-seq cells from another species (e.g., human pancreas) using a shared one-to-one orthologue gene space. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-cell-type-annotation))
- **cross-species-cell-type-assignment-from-motif-importance**: Use when you have cell-type-annotated CRE BED intervals from two species sharing homologous cell types (e.g. seven adult heart cell types in human and mouse) and need to classify cell-type-specific regulatory sequences or assign single cells across species using motif-based models. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-cell-type-assignment-from-motif-importance))
- **cross-species-cell-type-mapping**: Use when the reference scRNA-seq dataset and the query dataset come from different species (e.g. mouse reference, human query or cross-species pairs), so gene identifiers must first be mapped to orthologs before reference-based classification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-species-cell-type-mapping))
- **cross-species-cell-type-matching**: Use when matching/aligning cell types between two species from scRNA-seq count data (e.g., human–mouse, human–marmoset, mouse–marmoset cortex), especially when homologous gene lists are incomplete and protein-sequence-based gene similarity is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-cell-type-matching))
- **cross-species-chh-training-set-curation**: Use when constructing a CHH training set for a nanopore 5mC deep learning model where methylation-positive CHH samples are scarce and per-9-mer context balance between positive and negative classes is required. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-species-chh-training-set-curation))
- **cross-species-chip-seq-peak-liftover**: Use when orthologous non-coding regulatory elements (e.g., TF ChIP-seq peaks) must be lifted between species and naive chain-file liftOver suffers from multi-mapping, low precision, or low mapping rate. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-chip-seq-peak-liftover))
- **cross-species-conservation-comparison**: Use when you have predicted core promoter element records (position, score, TSS) for multiple species and want to test whether element positioning — e.g. TATA, dInr, PB relative to CAGE-annotated TSSs — is conserved across metazoans. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-conservation-comparison))
- **cross-species-contrastive-biomarker**: Dual-rule contrastive learning aligning mouse and human neural dynamics to discover cross-species biomarkers and retrospectively track clinical drug efficacy. Use when translating preclinical electrophysiology to human disease, building cross-domain neural latent spaces, or evaluating drug rescue in a human-anchored geometry. ([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/general-ml/cross-species-contrastive-biomarker) · ★4)
- **cross-species-cross-tissue-model-validation**: Use when a tissue-specific RNA modification predictor (e.g., im6A-TS-CNN on 41-nt m6A segments) must be shown to generalize across species-tissue combinations before deployment or comparison. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-cross-tissue-model-validation))
- **cross-species-dna-6ma-dataset-handling**: Use when raw labeled FASTA sequences (e.g., the cross-species DNA 6mA benchmark of 2,768 positive / 2,716 negative 41-nt samples) must be converted into fixed-length numeric k-mer frequency features for machine-learning classification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-dna-6ma-dataset-handling))
- **cross-species-embedding-integration-interpretation**: Use when trained cross-species cell embeddings exist and you need to judge cell-type-level alignment across species, e.g. homologous types (Vip, Sncg) mixing while species-specific types (Meis2, pericytes, SMC) stay separated. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-embedding-integration-interpretation))
- **cross-species-enhancer-classification-transfer**: Use when you have cell-type-specific CRE models trained in one species (e.g., mouse cardiomyocyte/erythroid) and want to classify homologous CREs from another species, or to test whether predictions depend on sequence alignment. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-enhancer-classification-transfer))
- **cross-species-epigenomic-region-mapping**: Use when mapping regulatory peaks (ChIP-seq, DNase-seq, ATAC-seq) across species and sequence-based liftOver alone yields multi-mappers or low precision, and matched orthologous epigenomic datasets are available for both species. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-epigenomic-region-mapping))
- **cross-species-expression-pattern-comparison**: Use when interpreting differential expression results from ExpressAnalyst across taxonomically diverse species lacking reference transcriptomes, where KO-based mapping could double-count homologous genes as separate DE ortholog groups. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-expression-pattern-comparison))
- **cross-species-gene-homolog-mapping**: Use when scRNA-seq gene identifiers come from a species (e.g., mouse) whose names must be mapped to homologs (e.g., human) before matching against a default L-R pair database such as dsCellNet's 5,744-pair list. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-gene-homolog-mapping))
- **cross-species-gene-matching-comparison**: Use when comparing how ortholog/gene matches from protein-embedding distances (ProtBERT) differ from BLAST sequence-alignment hits across two species, e.g. human vs mouse protein FASTA plus embeddings. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-gene-matching-comparison))
- **cross-species-gene-ortholog-mapping**: Use when training data and query data come from different species (e.g. mouse reference, human query) and cell type labels must be transferred across species via orthologous genes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-gene-ortholog-mapping))
- **cross-species-gene-orthology-mapping**: Use when bulk RNA-seq samples from one species (e.g., rat micro-dissected renal tubule segments, GSE56743) must be deconvolved with a single-cell reference from another species (e.g., mouse kidney scRNA-seq, GSE107585), which first requires homologous-gene mapping. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-gene-orthology-mapping))
- **cross-species-genome-coordinate-processing**: Use when building training data for RNA modification site prediction from RMBase v2.0 BED/GTF annotations across multiple species, requiring genome-coordinate extraction of flanking sequence windows. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-genome-coordinate-processing))
- **cross-species-genome-discrimination**: Use when raw ONT MinION fast5 reads must be assigned to one of several closely related species (e.g., Poeciliidae guppies) using only genome FASTAs, on inexpensive hardware without basecalling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-species-genome-discrimination))
- **cross-species-hot-region-comparison**: Use when DRIP-seq IP/control raw data (with RNaseH-treated or mock controls) are available for cell lines and C. elegans, and the goal is to test whether HOT regions are enriched for R-loops relative to occupancy-matched control regions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-hot-region-comparison))
- **cross-species-hot-region-count-validation**: Use when narrowPeak ChIP-seq peak files for multiple genomes (hg19, mm9, dm3, ce10) must be converted into HOT region BED tracks and per-species counts checked against published HOT-region numbers. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-hot-region-count-validation))
- **cross-species-lncrna-model-transfer-evaluation**: Use when you have per-species trained lncRNA classifiers and independent FASTA test sets from multiple species, and need to measure homologous vs. divergent transfer (e.g., animal vs. plant) via an MCC matrix. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-species-lncrna-model-transfer-evaluation))

([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tissue-protein-imputation-benchmarking))
- **cross-tissue-qval-variance-ranking**: Use when you have a multi-tissue single-cell atlas (e.g. blood, bone marrow, lymph node, lung) with a shared cell-type annotation and want to find pathway-level features unique to one tissue site rather than testing tissue pairs in isolation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tissue-qval-variance-ranking))
- **cross-tissue-sqtl-effect-size-correlation-analysis**: Use when you have per-tissue significant sQTL results (e.g. GTEx sqtls-*fdr.nominal.tsv tables) and need to quantify, cluster, or visualize cross-tissue sQTL sharing, especially to test whether brain tissues share sQTLs differently from non-brain tissues. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tissue-sqtl-effect-size-correlation-analysis))
- **cross-tokenizer-on-policy-distillation**: Cross-Tokenizer On-Policy Distillation framework using byte-prefix marginalization to enable knowledge transfer between models with different tokenizers while preserving policy quality. ([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/nlp-llm/cross-tokenizer-on-policy-distillation) · ★4)
- **Cross-Tool Workflow Orchestration**: Guide for coordinating work across Trello, Jira, and Confluence. Use when work spans multiple tools, when deciding which tool to use, or when linking work items across systems. ([ramirez-justin/claude-plugins](https://github.com/ramirez-justin/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/integration-plugin/skills/cross-tool-workflow))
- **cross-tool-action-items**: Find untracked commitments and action items across email, Slack, and issue trackers. Trigger on "what am I forgetting", "any action items I missed", "what's falling through the cracks", "am I dropping anything", "what did I promise to do". ([gpsandhu23/daily_productivity_plugin](https://github.com/gpsandhu23/daily_productivity_plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/cross-tool-action-items))
- **cross-tool-alignment-consistency-comparison**: Use when a 10x scRNA-seq sample has been analyzed for pathogen reads by more than one alignment strategy and you need to verify that EGFP+/pathogen+ cell calls and per-cell UMI counts are consistent across methods. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-alignment-consistency-comparison))
- **cross-tool-analytics-rigor**: Cross-tool product analytics (Mixpanel/Jira/Zendesk) with strict guardrails — confidence levels, n= minimums, attribution ceiling, proxy analysis before "unknown." Use for metric drops, funnel analysis, bug impact, user journey, or any question crossing analytics and issue-tracking data. ([meravdonyo-eng/claude-skills](https://github.com/meravdonyo-eng/claude-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/cross-tool-analytics-rigor) · ★1)
- **cross-tool-ani-accuracy-comparison**: Use when you need to quantitatively validate or compare ANI/strain-comparison accuracy of inStrain versus other tools (dRep, MIDAS, StrainPhlAn) using simulated genomes with known ground-truth ANI values. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-ani-accuracy-comparison))
- **cross-tool-annotation-agreement-quantification**: Use when you have 3' UTR predictions from multiple annotation tools plus a trusted canonical annotation (e.g. WormBase chrI GFF) and need to quantify which tool agrees with the reference. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-annotation-agreement-quantification))
- **cross-tool-annotation-benchmarking**: Use when you need to compare annotation pipelines head-to-head on million-cell datasets (&gt;1 million cells) and quantify accuracy together with resource metrics (training time, peak memory, GPU requirement) to derive cost-efficiency conclusions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-annotation-benchmarking))
- **cross-tool-annotation-comparison**: Use when benchmarking tandem MS/MS metabolite annotation: identical spectra splits and compound databases must be run through multiple tools and top-k retrieval rates compared side by side. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-annotation-comparison--36970f895527793d))
- **cross-tool-annotation-comparison**: Use when the same variant set has been annotated by multiple annotation tools (ANNOVAR, SnpEff, VEP) and you need to quantify agreement on HGVSc, HGVSp, coding impact, or transcript accessions against a clinical reference set. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-annotation-comparison--d667fd905959882a))
- **cross-tool-annotation-concordance-analysis**: Use when you have a table of network-based (MetDNA3 level 3) feature annotations plus independent tool annotations for the same features and need to compute per-tool or all-tools concordance percentages. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-annotation-concordance-analysis--91cb04dec47db3b5))
- **cross-tool-annotation-concordance-analysis**: Use when multiple variant annotation tools (ANNOVAR, SnpEff, VEP) have been run on the same VCF and you need to quantify HGVS/coding-impact disagreement, benchmark against ClinVar, or assess ACMG/PVS1 impact. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-annotation-concordance-analysis--9740348c90ab5f76))
- **cross-tool-annotation-overlap-comparison**: Decision support for choosing an annotation tool for MALDI/SM data: run when a user asks whether SpaMTP's annotation covers METASPACE/pySM results on TOF data, or needs per-database overlap statistics between two annotation pipelines. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-annotation-overlap-comparison))
- **cross-tool-barcode-overlap-analysis**: Use when you have barcode count tables produced by two or more pre-processing tools on the same FASTQ dataset and need to benchmark their detection concordance, quantify three-tool overlaps, and compare runtime/memory. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-barcode-overlap-analysis))
- **cross-tool-benchmark-comparison**: Use when two sequence-based variant-effect tools (e.g. DeepFun/Basset and DeepSEA) have scored the same SNP set across matched assays (CTCF, DNase-seq, H3K4me3, H3K9ac) and you need a quantitative agreement metric. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-benchmark-comparison))
- **cross-tool-benchmark-comparison**: Reference for comparing clustering quality of multiple spectral clustering tools on the same MS/MS dataset, using the HyperSpec paper's benchmarking metrics and workflow. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-benchmark-comparison))
- **cross-tool-benchmark-count-comparison**: Use when evaluating or benchmarking DMR-calling software with a same-sample replicate pair where no differential methylation should be detected, and you need comparable region counts across tools. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-benchmark-count-comparison))
- **cross-tool-benchmark-reproduction**: Use when a study requires reproducing a published tool-benchmark (same input dataset, multiple strain-comparison tools) with ROC/AUC and detection-count metrics, e.g. validating SynTracker's reported AUC 0.93 and 112 strain pairs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-benchmark-reproduction))
- **cross-tool-benchmark-table-construction**: Use when evaluating a prediction tool against published baselines on a shared benchmark set, needing a per-tool/per-condition metric table with verifiable reference counts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-benchmark-table-construction))
- **cross-tool-binning-benchmark-comparison**: Use when multiple viral binning tools must be compared on identical scaffold inputs with sorted BAM coverage, and a reproducible metrics table (F1, precision, redundant-protein counts) is the deliverable. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-binning-benchmark-comparison))
- **cross-tool-capability-benchmarking**: Use when selecting among gene-name/date correction tools: you need an apples-to-apples capability matrix covering multi-file processing, dd/mm/yy vs mixed date formats, and HGNC name updating. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-capability-benchmarking))
- **cross-tool-cell-call-overlap-analysis**: Use when validating a new pathogen-detection tool for 10x scRNAseq against established tools by measuring concordance of infected-cell calls and correlating per-cell pathogen read/UMI counts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-cell-call-overlap-analysis))
- **cross-tool-chromatin-loop-reproducibility-comparison**: Use when comparing chromatin loop callers on identical ChIA-PET replicates — e.g. quantifying whether one pipeline's loop calls are more reproducible between replicates or recover another tool's calls. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-chromatin-loop-reproducibility-comparison))
- **cross-tool-circrna-quantification-comparison**: Use when comparing alternative circRNA quantification tools on the same ribo− RNA-seq data to decide whether a CIRCscore-style normalized ratio is more accurate than CLR-style back-splice counting, e.g. for loci with RT-qPCR validation values. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-circrna-quantification-comparison))
- **cross-tool-classification-agreement-analysis**: Use when you have per-read classification files from several metagenomic classifiers run on the same read set and need to measure pairwise or reference-based agreement (e.g. Jaccard-based rates) rather than accuracy against ground truth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-classification-agreement-analysis))
- **cross-tool-clustering-benchmarking**: Use when you have cluster assignments from multiple spectrum clustering tools plus peptide-spectrum match identifications, and need to compare clustering purity/completeness or cluster size distributions at equivalent error rates. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-clustering-benchmarking))
- **cross-tool-clustering-fragmentation-comparison**: Use when comparing multiple spectrum clustering tools' outputs on the same dataset and you need a fragmentation metric: the number of unique clusters a chosen peptide's spectra are split into per tool. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-clustering-fragmentation-comparison))
- **cross-tool-comparative-performance-evaluation**: Use when choosing among metagenomic fecal source tracking tools or claiming a new tool outperforms existing ones: run all candidates on the same benchmark samples with known source mixing proportions and tabulate sensitivity/specificity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-comparative-performance-evaluation))
- **cross-tool-comparison-evaluation**: Use when evaluating a malignant cell annotation method against published baselines on shared gold/silver standard scRNA-seq datasets, or when benchmarking inference runtime and memory scaling with dataset size. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-comparison-evaluation))
- **cross-tool-comparison-intersection**: Use when per-sample metagenomic assemblies have been profiled by both SynTracker (APSS) and inStrain (popANI), and the goal is to detect species whose within-species diversity is preferentially point-mutation- or structurally-driven. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-comparison-intersection))
- **cross-tool-comparison-metrics-tabulation**: Use when comparing genome-reconstruction performance of viral nanopore assembly pipelines (ViMOP, INSaFLU-TELEVIR, VirDetector) on the same read sets and you need a consolidated benchmark comparison table. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-comparison-metrics-tabulation))
- **cross-tool-complementarity-analysis**: Use when multiple gene-prioritization tools have been run on the same benchmark cases and you need per-tool unique top-k hit counts and evidence of complementarity across tools. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-complementarity-analysis))
- **cross-tool-computational-performance-comparison**: Use when reproducing or extending a runtime/speedup claim, e.g. quantifying how much faster a parallel pipeline is than its sequential version and whether it matches a tool's runtime on the same input dataset. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-computational-performance-comparison))
- **cross-tool-concordance-comparison-homer-vs-dchic**: Use when you need to validate a differential compartment caller by comparing its significant bin calls against an orthogonal tool (e.g. HOMER) on the same paired Hi-C conditions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-concordance-comparison-homer-vs-dchic))
- **cross-tool-continuity-swarm**: Use when work must move between AI coding tools, survive a paused session, be resumed from a verified checkpoint, or be prepared as a one-command Claude Code continuation. Capture local-only session lineage, audit portable project state, capture evidence, prepare and review a bounded handoff, record synchronization, generate a launch prompt, and resume only after current verification. ([Openly-Useful/agent-workflow-swarms](https://github.com/Openly-Useful/agent-workflow-swarms/tree/HEAD/skills/cross-tool-continuity-swarm) · ★1)
- **cross-tool-core-genome-comparison**: Use when comparing pangenome/core-genome predictions from multiple tools (mOTUpan, Roary, PPanGGOLiN) on the same genome sets, especially MAGs spanning a wide completeness range. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-core-genome-comparison))
- **cross-tool-correction-benchmarking**: Use when comparing self-correction tools on real long-read datasets (PacBio S. cerevisiae, ONT D. melanogaster, ONT H. sapiens chr 1) and need a reproducible table of correction quality and resource metrics. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-correction-benchmarking))
- **cross-tool-dataframe-equality-checking**: Use when an mzML file has been converted to Parquet by arcMS (convert_mzml_to_parquet()) and you must verify that pandas read_parquet() reproduces exactly the DEIMoS deimos.load() DataFrame from the same mzML. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-dataframe-equality-checking))
- **cross-tool-detection-performance-comparison**: Use when comparing detection performance (FDR, TPR) of longitudinal high-dimensional tools like LongDat and MaAsLin2 across varying numbers of covariates on simulated microbiome count data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-detection-performance-comparison))
- **cross-tool-differential-overlap-analysis**: Use when a pixel × ion feature matrix (e.g., 14,260 pixels × 3,044 ion peaks, AFAD-ESI positive mode) has differential analysis results from one spatial metabolomics tool and you need to cross-validate them against an independent tool's clustering and differential calls. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-differential-overlap-analysis))
- **cross-tool-dmr-comparison-analysis**: Use when benchmarking DMR-calling tools for false-positive rates (e.g., contrasting replicates of the same sample should produce zero DMRs) or reproducing the DMRfinder vs. DSS/bsseq comparison. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-dmr-comparison-analysis))
- **cross-tool-driver-prediction-comparison**: Use when comparing multiple driver-gene prediction tools' gene lists against known driver sets (e.g. CGC 616 genes, NCG 711 genes, PMH 501 genes) over matched datasets, and recall-weighted performance must be quantified and ranked. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-driver-prediction-comparison))
- **cross-tool-enrichment-performance-comparison**: Use when choosing among metabolomics pathway enrichment tools on runtime grounds, or when replicating the paper's runtime comparison of EnrichMet vs MetaboAnalystR vs FELLA on a fixed metabolite list. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-enrichment-performance-comparison))
- **cross-tool-epigenomic-pipeline-execution**: Use when scChIP-seq or scATAC-seq count matrices with known ground-truth labels are available and the goal is to reproduce or extend a multi-method benchmark comparing clustering performance via adjusted Rand index. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-epigenomic-pipeline-execution))
- **cross-tool-error-frequency-tabulation**: Use when comparing de novo peptide sequencing predictions (e.g. pNovo 3, SMSNet, PointNovo, Casanovo) against pseudo-ground-truth PSMs and you need to characterize which error types dominate each tool's incorrect predictions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-error-frequency-tabulation))
- **cross-tool-error-overlap-analysis**: Use when two error/variant callers (e.g., CRAQ and SyRI) have been run on the same draft assembly and you need to compute what fraction of one tool's breakpoints fall inside the other's SVs, and categorize the unique calls. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-error-overlap-analysis))
- **cross-tool-execution-time-comparison**: Use when comparing computational efficiency (CPU time and wall-clock time) of multiple pathogen-quantification pipelines for scRNAseq data, and a measured ranking must be reproduced on an identical input sample. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-execution-time-comparison))
- **cross-tool-exon-prediction-comparison**: Use when comparing exon/IEB prediction tools (EXFI, ChopStitch, GMAP) on the same transcriptomes and WGS reads, needing precision/recall/F1 against a GFF3 reference plus exon mappability via BWA MEM. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-exon-prediction-comparison))
- **cross-tool-expression-benchmarking**: Use when you need to validate a long-read transcript quantification workflow by measuring replicate-to-replicate Pearson correlations (e.g. &gt;0.971) and cross-tool agreement with an alternative quantifier such as LIQA (~0.83), as in Fig. 5g / Supplementary Table 4. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-expression-benchmarking))
- **cross-tool-factor-similarity-correlation-index**: Use when several cell-cell communication scoring methods (e.g., CellChat, CellPhoneDB, NATMI, SingleCellSignalR, rank-based cell2cell scores) have been applied to the same multi-sample dataset and you need to assess whether Tensor-cell2cell decompositions agree across tools. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-factor-similarity-correlation-index))
- **cross-tool-feature-comparison**: Use when multiple LC-MS processing tools (asari, XCMS, MZmine with wavelet or local-minimum peak detection) report very different feature counts on the same dataset and you must determine whether the extra features are genuine or low-quality non-reproducible peaks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-feature-comparison))
- **cross-tool-feature-count-comparison**: Decision support for selecting an LC/MS signal-detection/peak-picking tool: you have raw mzML files, candidate peak-detection algorithms (e.g. mzLearn, XCMS, ASARI), and need objective per-dataset feature counts plus TP/FP rates to compare them. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-feature-count-comparison))
- **cross-tool-feature-mapping-comparison**: Use when you need to benchmark a peak-picking/grouping workflow against an established tool like MetaboAnalyst on the same raw NMR spectra, and must quantify differences in feature resolution and overlap of statistically significant features. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-feature-mapping-comparison))
- **cross-tool-feature-matching-benchmarking**: Use when reproducing published untargeted metabolomics feature detection with a different tool and quantifying what fraction of the published (XCMS/CAMERA) features is recovered, separately per ionization mode, from public MetaboLights raw data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-feature-matching-benchmarking))
- **cross-tool-file-size-benchmarking**: Use when choosing between two visualization tools for large ordination datasets: generate equivalent outputs from both on the same samples and metadata, then measure and compare total output file size on disk. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-file-size-benchmarking))
- **cross-tool-flow**: Declarative YAML/JSON authoring surface over dispatcher-core (Wave 2 surface, Wave 1 engine). Lets the user, Margot or Scribe define multi-step flows chaining Linear, Gmail, Calendar, Margot and Composio MCP tools, and run them via Dispatcher. Use to author, validate or run a flow file in Pi-Dev-Ops/flows/ with ordered steps, templated inputs, tool allowlists, and manual, telegram_command, cron or webhook triggers, including the Telegram /flow command or POST /api/flows/{name}/run. ([CleanExpo/Pi-Dev-Ops](https://github.com/CleanExpo/Pi-Dev-Ops/tree/HEAD/skills/cross-tool-flow) · ★1)
- **cross-tool-formula-accuracy-benchmarking**: Use when you have a labeled MS/MS challenge dataset (e.g., CASMI 2022, 304 positive-mode spectra) and need to compare formula-annotation accuracy of two or more tools under identical inputs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-formula-accuracy-benchmarking))
- **cross-tool-formula-annotation-comparison**: Use when evaluating whether a data-driven formula prediction model (e.g., a formula transformer trained on MS/MS spectra) outperforms a classical tree-based competitor on the same spectra, and you need accuracy, coverage, and concordance metrics on identical inputs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-formula-annotation-comparison))
- **cross-tool-fragmentation-prediction-evaluation**: Use when comparing MS/MS spectral prediction quality across in silico fragmentation tools (e.g., FIORA vs ICEBERG vs CFM-ID) and assessing generalization to compounds structurally dissimilar from the training set via sqrt-weighted cosine similarity stratified by maximum Tanimoto similarity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-fragmentation-prediction-evaluation))
- **cross-tool-integration-consistency-analysis**: Use when the same MRM acquisition dataset (e.g., 113 Skyline small-molecule test files) has been processed by two tools and you must verify that quantification results (peak areas per transition such as Drug_light/Drug_heavy) agree within a defined correlation threshold. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-integration-consistency-analysis))
- **cross-tool-isoform-abundance-comparison**: Use when a single-locus long-read splicing assay (e.g. a minigene) has been aligned once and you need to compare how two isoform callers (VIsoQLR vs StringTie2) disagree on isoform counts, abundances, and isoform lengths. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-isoform-abundance-comparison))
- **cross-tool-jaccard-distance-computation**: Use when you have per-dataset, per-tool significant-feature result tables and need to measure how much different DA tools agree (or disagree) on which ASVs are significantly differentially abundant, including under different prevalence-filtering regimes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/cross-tool-jaccard-distance-computation))
- **cross-tool-m6a-benchmarking**: Use when evaluating which nanopore-based differential RNA modification caller detects m6A stoichiometry changes between two multi-replicate conditions, e.g. WT vs methyltransferase knockout. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-m6a-benchmarking))
- **cross-tool-metabolite-prediction-comparison**: Decision support for choosing among CYP450 SoM or byproduct predictors: when a practitioner has a common evaluation test set (e.g., EBoMD2, the BioTransformer hold-out 13321_2018_324_MOESM6_ESM.sdf, or the HDFAME 60Reactants. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-metabolite-prediction-comparison))
- **cross-tool-metagenomic-distance-normalization**: Use when comparing multiple metagenome distance tools (Libra, Mash, Simka) on the same sample set to assess how data reduction, read-count normalization, or presence/absence metrics distort clustering by a known metadata factor such as body site. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-metagenomic-distance-normalization))
- **cross-tool-methylation-prediction-comparison**: Use when nanopore fast5 data from a controlled methylation mixture (e.g. dataset 1 with 100 selected CpG sites) must be processed through multiple methylation callers and their per-site predictions compared quantitatively. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-methylation-prediction-comparison))
- **cross-tool-motif-prediction-alignment**: Use when comparing motif discovery tools on datasets with known implanted motifs (length-6 IUPAC/degenerate or exact strings) at controlled frequencies, and a nucleotide-level recovery metric such as nCC is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-motif-prediction-alignment))
- **cross-tool-nucleosome-caller-benchmarking**: Use when comparing nucleosome-calling tools on identical chromatin accessibility data — e.g., deciding whether RoboCOP run on ATAC-seq alone suffices versus NucleoATAC, or whether RoboCOP's MNase+ATAC profile outperforms DANPOS2 — by nucleosome-position AUPR on a shared validation set. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-nucleosome-caller-benchmarking))
- **cross-tool-parameter-benchmarking**: Use when two or more parameter-optimization tools (e.g., AutoTuner, IPO) have produced centWave parameter sets for the same raw LC-MS dataset and the analysis goal is to compare how each choice changes feature counts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-parameter-benchmarking))
- **cross-tool-peak-area-correlation-analysis**: Use when two or more GC–MS peak-picking or deconvolution tools (e.g. eRah, XCMS centWave, MetAlign) have produced per-sample quantification tables and you need to quantify their agreement by correlating peak areas/intensities per compound across samples. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-peak-area-correlation-analysis))
- **cross-tool-peak-count-benchmarking**: Use when a novel peak-picking pipeline for untargeted LC/HRMS data needs quantitative benchmarking against MZmine 2, xcms-CAMERA, or MS-DIAL on a shared raw test file. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-peak-count-benchmarking))
- **cross-tool-performance-comparison**: Use when choosing a CC peak caller for large calling cards datasets, or when reproducing Fig. 2f-g style runtime/memory scaling benchmarks across tools on downsampled qbed data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-performance-comparison))
- **cross-tool-performance-comparison**: Use when a new signature-refitting tool claims speed advantages and you need a controlled same-input runtime comparison against baseline tools, recording per-tool elapsed wall-clock times and ratios. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-performance-comparison))
- **cross-tool-performance-comparison**: Use when selecting a FASTQ preprocessing or QC tool on speed grounds: you must benchmark wall-clock time and reads/s of fastp vs FASTQC, Cutadapt, SOAPnuke, AfterQC and Trimmomatic in SE and PE modes on identical input, all single-threaded. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/cross-tool-performance-comparison))
- **cross-tool-performance-comparison**: Use when when you have raw tandem MS metabolomics data in vendor formats (. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-performance-comparison) · 2 copies)
- **cross-tool-performance-comparison**: Use when comparing computation time of a new GPU-accelerated implementation against its CPU predecessor on the same workstation, inputs, and settings (e.g. cudaMMC vs 3D-GNOME on GM12878 CTCF ChIA-PET data). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-performance-comparison--d7085a688a90c8be))
- **cross-tool-performance-comparison**: Use when comparing the runtime and RAM footprint of ORIGINS, LandSCENT, and CytoTRACE on the same GEO scRNA-seq count matrices to reproduce or extend the paper's Table 1 performance benchmark. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-performance-comparison--5615a04dd2bbb395))
- **cross-tool-performance-comparison-analysis**: Use when comparing the computational cost (wall time, peak memory) of alternative Hi-C compartment-calling tools on the same binned contact maps at multiple resolutions or down-sampling depths. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-performance-comparison-analysis))
- **cross-tool-performance-ratio-analysis**: Use when comparing mapping throughput/speed of raw-signal nanopore mappers across tools on the same FAST5 datasets and reference genomes, e.g., verifying whether each tool exceeds nanopore sequencing throughput on the human genome. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-performance-ratio-analysis))
- **cross-tool-prediction-comparison**: Use when two plasmid-prediction tools have been run on identical contig/fragment sets and you must quantify error rates, agreement, and plasmid:chromosome separation ratios to decide which classifier to trust. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-prediction-comparison))
- **cross-tool-prediction-comparison**: Use when you hold a labeled RNA transcript FASTA test set (e.g., H-Test mRNAs vs lncRNAs) and need to verify or replicate reported accuracy differences across multiple lncRNA-coding-potential predictors rather than run just one tool. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-prediction-comparison))
- **cross-tool-prediction-discrepancy-analysis**: Use when the same set of MS/MS spectra has been annotated by two independent classifiers (e.g. CANOPUS and MolNetEnhancer) and you need to tabulate per-class disagreement before choosing one tool's output for downstream scoring such as NPClassScore. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-prediction-discrepancy-analysis))
- **cross-tool-prediction-overlap-analysis**: Use when a task requires benchmarking phasiRNA prediction tools by comparing the number and overlap of predicted 21-PHAS genes from multiple tools on the same small RNA-seq dataset with a shared reference sequence. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-prediction-overlap-analysis))
- **cross-tool-prediction-performance-comparison**: Use when a new in silico reactant-prediction or site-of-metabolism tool must be benchmarked against CypReact, ADMET Predictor, or SMARTCyp on the 1632-molecule CYP450 reactant dataset using per-enzyme AUROC. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-prediction-performance-comparison))
- **cross-tool-probability-collection**: Use when benchmarking two or more RNA editing detection tools on a shared bona fide site set and you need per-site predictions aligned by genomic position to compute head-to-head ROC curves, AUC, or FNR. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-probability-collection))
- **cross-tool-processing-time-comparison**: Use when comparing the scalability/runtime of MRM quantitation or batch-effect correction tools on an identical large-scale targeted metabolomics dataset. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-processing-time-comparison))
- **cross-tool-pseudotime-correlation-analysis**: Use when you need to benchmark or validate trajectory-inference consistency across multiple pseudotime tools on a preprocessed plant scRNA-seq Seurat object with known root-state cell types (e.g., meristem). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-pseudotime-correlation-analysis))
- **cross-tool-quantification-comparison**: Use when comparing the runtime or throughput of competing read-counting/quantification tools on the same SAM/BAM alignment files and reference annotations, e.g. to decide whether a specialized counter's overhead is acceptable. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-quantification-comparison))
- **cross-tool-research**: Find, correlate, summarize, and investigate information across the user’s connectors. Use for cross-tool searches, conversation or ticket summaries, and investigations that need alerts, logs, messages, documents, or code context. ([tines/skills](https://github.com/tines/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/cross-tool-research) · ★1)
- **cross-tool-result-aggregation**: Use when several annotation tools have been run on the same scRNA-seq train/test splits and their per-cell predictions must be harmonized, scored against study labels, and aggregated into a single comparison table. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-result-aggregation))
- **cross-tool-result-concordance-analysis**: Use when you have executed multiple NPDtools database search pipelines (Dereplicator, VarQuest, Dereplicator+, or MetaMiner in different modes) on identical test spectra or RiPP sequence inputs and need to understand their relative sensitivity, specificity, and complementarity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-result-concordance-analysis) · 3 copies)
- **cross-tool-retention-comparison**: Use when you need to quantify and compare false-removal rates (off-target loss of bacterial reads) of host-decontamination tools such as Hostile vs HRRT on simulated bacterial Illumina data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-retention-comparison))
- **cross-tool-review**: Independent review by the other tool, automatically at spine steps 3 and 6 and before a freeform delivery's push, or explicitly for an ad-hoc review. Verify and prune the findings. ([Ellebam/slop-meister](https://github.com/Ellebam/slop-meister/tree/HEAD/dist/claude/skills/cross-tool-review) · ★2 · 3 copies)
- **cross-tool-runtime-benchmark-tabulation**: Use when comparing the IGHV-reconstruction completeness or runtime of competing BCR reconstruction tools across the same set of RNA-seq samples, e.g. reproducing the CRIS vs V'DJer/TRUST/MiXCR benchmark on GSE66228 CLL samples. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-runtime-benchmark-tabulation))
- **cross-tool-runtime-benchmarking**: Use when comparing batch-integration methods on the same datasets and you must report both integration quality (iLISI/cLISI/RNN) and wall-clock runtime per tool in a single comparison CSV. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-runtime-benchmarking))
- **cross-tool-runtime-comparison**: Use when a time-course regulon analysis must be made feasible on commodity hardware and you need measured per-regulon wall-clock times for Genexpi (OpenCL/GPU) versus TD-Aracne before choosing one. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-runtime-comparison))
- **cross-tool-runtime-comparison**: Use when comparing computational cost (runtime and peak RSS) of multiple SV-calling tools or pipeline variants on a shared dataset, e.g. Blackbird SLR/hybrid vs Sniffles2 and PBSV on HG002. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-runtime-comparison))
- **cross-tool-runtime-comparison**: Use when you need to quantify and compare prediction speed of competing MS/MS spectral prediction tools on the same compound set, including the effect of GPU vs CPU execution. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cross-tool-runtime-comparison))
- **cross-tool-runtime-profiling-on-hpc**: Use when benchmarking multiple phylogenomic marker-mining tools (e.g. Patchwork vs ALiBaSeq vs aTRAM 2) for both accuracy and runtime across a sequencing-coverage gradient on a Slurm-managed HPC cluster. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-runtime-profiling-on-hpc))

([baneeishaque/ai-suite](https://github.com/baneeishaque/ai-suite/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/general/permission-sync/cross-tool-shell-command-permission-sync))
- **cross-tool-significance-overlap-quantification**: Decision support for choosing between longitudinal differential-abundance tools when multiple covariates (e.g. medications) may confound a time/treatment effect in high-dimensional microbiome data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-significance-overlap-quantification))
- **cross-tool-skill-manager**: 当需在多个 AI 编码工具（Cursor/Claude/Agents/Windsurf/Copilot/Codex/Cline/Aider/Continue/Roo/Augment）间查看、新建、编辑、启停、复制、移动或删除本地技能与规则文件时使用；产出针对各工具正确路径与格式的命令操作；不适用于工具私有只读插件缓存或线上技能市场。触发词：管理技能、跨工具复制技能、列出我的技能。 ([findscripter/everything-skills](https://github.com/findscripter/everything-skills/tree/HEAD/00-meta/cross-tool-skill-manager) · ★11 · 2 copies)
- **cross-tool-speedup-factor-comparison**: Use when reproducing or extending runtime/speedup benchmarks of SCEVAN against CopyKAT, inferCNV, DNAcopy, or CNVkit on scRNA-seq count matrices, timing classification and segmentation stages separately. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cross-tool-speedup-factor-comparison))
- **cross-tool-speedup-ratio-comparison**: Use when benchmarking raw signal mappers head-to-head (RawHash2 vs RawHash/UNCALLED/Sigmap) on R9.4.1 FAST5 data and you need speedup ratios (26.5x/19.2x/4.0x) or sequencing-length reduction (~1.9x). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cross-tool-speedup-ratio-comparison))
- **cross-tool-speedup-ratio-computation**: Use when you need to quantify how much faster (and more memory-frugal) one sequence-analysis tool is than baselines on the same input, e.g. validating MAPseq's ~10x vs NINJA-OPS and ~100x vs VSEARCH speedups. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/cross-tool-speedup-ratio-computation))
- **cross-tool-splicing-statistics-comparison**: Use when IsoTools (long-read LRTS) differential splicing results need independent validation against a short-read tool such as rMATS, and a quantitative agreement metric (Spearman correlation) is required. - **cross-tool-taxonomic-benchmarking**: Use when a newly proposed MAG/prokaryotic genome taxonomy classifier must be validated against established gold standards (GTDBtk, CAMITAX) on a common benchmark set with known NCBI taxonomic labels. - **cross-tool-te-marker-benchmarking**: Use when evaluating whether a locus-resolved TE quantification tool outperforms a family-level TE tool for detecting cell-type marker TEs in scRNA-Seq clusters. - **cross-validate**: Use when doing coding, kube/cluster, or other consequential operational work with two or more models available, and when the user asks for cross-validation, a second opinion, or an ensemble review.
